### [id: mcp-standard-001] MCP as the standard integration layer for agents (stateless core, Apps, Tasks)
- alias: Model Context Protocol, MCP Apps, Tasks extension, spec 2026-07-28
- stage: accelerating
- confidence: high
- first_observed: 2026-06-11
- last_evidence: 2026-09-20
- evidence:
  - 2026-09-20 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23809 — cs.SE authors, "Packaged, But Not Portable: Why Conforming to the Agent Plugin Standard Is Rare, and Why Conforming Would Not Be Enough" (abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session, found via the cs.SE arXiv-rotation exploration slot): the first rigorous, large-N empirical adoption measurement of the Agent Plugins v1.0.0 standard already tracked here (08-06 evidence line) — builds AgentPluginZoo, a provenance-tracked corpus of 68,072 plugin bundles across 30,655 repositories, finding only 6.2% conform to the spec, and arguing conformance alone would not be sufficient for cross-agent portability given how bundles are actually composed. A further sobering empirical coverage/quality datapoint on this trend's own ecosystem, in the same vein as Kraishan's MCP registry census and Cohere's ATE dataset — this time at the plugin-packaging layer rather than the MCP-server or task-coverage layers. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-12 → 09-20); dropped the oldest evidence line (OpenAI Agents SDK docs, 06-11 — the trend's original founding-adoption anchor, long superseded by the denser recent production/quality evidence) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; a further quality/coverage reality-check, not a new gate).
  - 2026-09-12 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.14119 — Obada Kraishan, "Same Name, Different Server: A Security Census of Silent Drift in the Model Context Protocol Ecosystem" (cs.SE, abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session — resolves a queue item first title-scanned 09-15, opened and verified today): harvests the FULL public MCP registry (21,643 servers, 72,606 version records, August 2026 snapshot), scans source for 14,353 servers against an 8-class threat catalogue calibrated against 414 hand-labeled findings. Corrected high-severity prevalence ~7.6%; the central finding is INSTABILITY — 51.1% of multi-version servers changed what they advertise between versions, 40.6% silently, 4.2% redirected their remote endpoint to a different host while keeping registry identity (a change the protocol never surfaces to clients); silent drift correlates with nearly 3x higher odds of a high-severity finding (OR=2.96), and popularity is a poor safety proxy (OR=0.78/log-star). A rigorous, large-N empirical registry-level census — a further sobering coverage/quality datapoint on the ecosystem this trend tracks by adoption metrics, in the same vein as Cohere's ATE dataset below but at the SECURITY/STABILITY layer rather than task-coverage. Single new independent primary (solo author, but the ≥3-source bar is only for creating a trend, not adding evidence) → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-07 → 09-12); dropped the oldest evidence line (EMA-stable, 06-18 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; a further quality/coverage reality-check, not a new gate). Cross-link: agent-security-004.

A concrete instance of the trend's own 07-29-registered watch item ("real-world Tier-1 SDK migration to the stateless core") from a major vendor already tracked on this trend (distinct artifact from the earlier Cloudflare Sandbox/OS evidence on agent-sandbox-007).

Also reports scale context: "close to half-a-billion downloads a month" across Tier-1 SDKs, both TS and Python SDKs past 1 BILLION total downloads. Fires the trend's standing "Tier 1 SDK adoption speed" watch item — all four official SDKs simultaneously in beta for the 07-28 spec
- notes: Stage accelerating despite the conservative no-history rule: adoption documented in ≥3 independent orgs' official frameworks (MCP project spec/SDKs, OpenAI Agents SDK, Google ADK). Direction: from local tool-wiring protocol to production infrastructure (stateless, OAuth, UI, long-running tasks). Confidence HIGH since W29 (≥2 independent authoritative primaries + sustained multi-week confirmation). ### [id: pd-disagg-002] Prefill/decode disaggregation as the standard LLM serving architecture
- alias: PD disaggregation, disaggregated prefilling, disaggregated serving, KV transfer, attention-FFN disaggregation, AFD
- stage: accelerating
- confidence: high
- first_observed: 2026-06-11
- last_evidence: 2026-09-21
- evidence:
  - 2026-09-10 — https://blog.vllm.ai/blog/2026-09-10-tiered-kv-offloading — official vLLM blog, "Tiered KV Cache Offloading in vLLM" (opened this session via a direct slug-grep of the vLLM blog index): a host-centric design (all KV data flows through host DRAM before cascading to secondary tiers — filesystem, object storage, remote peers) that preserves evicted KV data instead of recomputing it, making KV data SHAREABLE across nodes — warm-starting new instances from shared storage and transferring KV data between peers "for disaggregated serving or load balancing." A concrete serving-engine capacity/sharing mechanism directly enabling the trend's own disaggregated-serving thesis (cross-node KV transfer as a first-class, documented feature, not a research proposal) — available in vLLM since v0.22, now given a dedicated engineering writeup. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-09 → 09-10); dropped the oldest line (vLLM's Kimi K3 day-0 blog, 07-27 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/medium; reinforcement, not a new gate).
  - 2026-09-09 — https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/when-to-use-encode-prefill-decode-disaggregation-to-accelerate-multimodal-model-serving/ — NVIDIA official Technical Blog, "When to Use Encode-Prefill-Decode Disaggregation to Accelerate Multimodal Model Serving" (opened this session via the tier-i Atom-feed sweep): NVIDIA Dynamo implements ENCODE-prefill-decode (EPD) disaggregation — a THREE-way split separating vision-encoder compute from LLM prefill/decode for multimodal inference — reporting up to 5x faster time-to-first-token and 7x faster end-to-end response for image-heavy/short-output/quantized-MoE workloads, with three named encoder-placement topologies (aggregated, colocated, disaggregated-via-NIXL) and concrete guidance on when EPD helps vs. hurts (gains shrink when decode dominates or dense models reduce the encoder's compute share). A SECOND independent engine/vendor (after vLLM's Model Runner V2 ENCODER/PREFILL/DECODE split, cited below) converging on the same three-way disaggregation axis, this time with a full production-guidance writeup (topology choice, NVFP4 quantization tradeoffs) rather than a release-notes mention — corroborates EPD as a real, engine-agnostic extension of the trend's core PD-disaggregation thesis into multimodal serving. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 08-26 → 09-09); dropped the two oldest lines (ELDR, 07-01, and the vLLM AFD Plugin blog post, 07-23 — both still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/medium; reinforcement, not a new gate).
  - 2026-09-08 — https://blog.vllm.ai/blog/2026-09-08-vllm-agentx — official vLLM blog, "vLLM on AgentX" (opened this session via a direct slug-grep of the vLLM blog index — closing a genuine multi-post coverage gap the last several dailies missed, see today's report): vLLM's coordinated approach to agentic-workload serving — KV cache management, parallelism/engine optimizations, and PD-disaggregation methodologies together — measured on SemiAnalysis's public AgentX/InferenceX benchmark: up to 130K total tokens per GPU-second on DeepSeek V4 Pro, 376 tok/s interactivity on MiniMax M3, and a 14.6x-106x serving-cost advantage over Opus 5 API pricing across DeepSeek V4 Pro/MiniMax M3/Kimi K3. A concrete, benchmarked instance of PD disaggregation as one pillar of a coordinated agentic-serving stack (not a standalone feature) from the trend's most-cited serving engine. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add. NO stage move (accelerating/medium; reinforcement).
  - 2026-08-26 — https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/releases/tag/v0.28.0 — vLLM v0.28.0 release notes (opened this session via WebFetch, direct GitHub access; PyPI version bump 0.27.1→0.28.0 had been logged as version-signal-only on 08-27, content now opened): Model Runner V2 adds ENCODER/PREFILL/DECODE disaggregation support (a THREE-way split, extending past the standard prefill/decode boundary the same way AFD did) alongside weight offloading and multi-layer MTP KV-cache support. A concrete stable-engine maturation of the trend's own disaggregation axis, from the same release also cited on subquad-attn-012 (DeepSeek V4 sparse MLA) — one artifact, two independent on-axis claims. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 08-24 → 08-26); dropped oldest evidence (vLLM v0.24.0 GA, 06-29 — superseded by this same engine's own v0.28.0 advance) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/medium; reinforcement, not a new gate).
  - 2026-08-24 — https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/how-nvidia-groq-3-lpx-unlocks-ultrafast-interactivity-at-long-context-on-nvidia-vera-rubin/ — NVIDIA official Technical Blog, "How NVIDIA Groq 3 LPX Unlocks Ultrafast Interactivity at Long Context on NVIDIA Vera Rubin" (opened this session via the tier-i lab sweep): a new accelerator (Groq 3 LPX) paired with the Vera Rubin NVL72 platform reports a first third-party benchmark (Artificial Analysis, 100K-context, Gemma 4 31B) of 3,431 output tokens/second, and documents that it "supports multiple co-execution configurations with Vera Rubin NVL72, such as prefill-decode disaggregation, attention-FFN disaggregation, and speculative external-drafter decoding, scaling to multi-trillion parameter models." A NEW independent primary (NVIDIA, not previously an author of its own dedicated evidence line on this trend, only a DCP co-author) confirming PD-disaggregation and AFD are now default, hardware-vendor-supported co-execution modes on frontier accelerator silicon, not just serving-engine software features — a hardware-layer corroboration of the trend's software-layer maturity. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 08-07 → 08-24); dropped oldest evidence (official SGLang PD-disaggregation docs, 06-11 — the original SGLang datapoint, superseded by the denser recent engine/hardware evidence) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/medium; reinforcement, not a new gate).
  - 2026-08-07 — https://blog.vllm.ai/blog/2026-08-07-decode-context-parallelism — official vLLM engine blog, "Efficient Decode Context Parallelism with vLLM for Long Context Workloads" (Seonghee Lee, Sungsoo Ha, Omri Almog [NVIDIA], Lucas Wilkinson [Red Hat AI]; opened this session, found via the vLLM blog slug-grep method): introduces Decode Context Parallelism (DCP) — shards the KV cache across GPUs by SEQUENCE dimension at decode time (distinct from tensor/pipeline parallelism), reporting up to 3× higher throughput on long-context agentic workloads vs standard tensor parallelism; ships for both MLA and GQA attention backends, offline and online usage documented. A NEW independent serving technique (two orgs, NVIDIA + Red Hat AI) on this trend's broader KV-cache/context-parallelism axis — long-context serving efficiency via cache sharding rather than the compute-bound/memory-bound PD split specifically, echoing how AFD extended the trend past the original prefill/decode boundary.
  - 2026-09-21 — https://blog.vllm.ai/blog/2026-09-21-qwen38-pd-serving — official vLLM blog, "PD Serving of Qwen3.8-2.4T" (opened this session via a direct slug-grep of the vLLM blog index): a full pareto-frontier PD-serving study on a GB300 NVL72 cluster (an 8K/1K workload) reaching 5,000 total tokens/GPU throughput and 180 generated tokens/user at low latency, with a fully documented KV-cache-capacity-driven tuning methodology (not just final numbers) explicitly generalized as reusable for tuning PD serving on any model. A further concrete, benchmarked engineering artifact from the trend's most-cited serving engine, on new-generation hardware (GB300, after the Qwen3.5 25K-TPS study on prior hardware). Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-10 → 09-21); dropped the oldest evidence line (DOPS, 2607.25498, 07-28 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; reinforcement, not a new gate).
  - 2026-08-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04502 — Chengyu Qiu, Xiao Fu, Fengcun Li, Yulei Qian, Yuchen Xie, Xunliang Cai, Yingdi Shan, Yongwei Wu, Mingxing Zhang (9 authors, Meituan/LongCat-line), "AFD-Ledger: Deployment Provisioning for Attention–FFN Disaggregation" (cs.DC, arXiv abstract + metadata via export.arxiv this session): an offline analytical provisioning system that answers, for a given model/workload/TPOT-SLO/hardware budget, whether AFD actually beats the best collocated deployment — jointly optimizes hardware assignment across both architectures (68.8–83.5% fewer deployment evaluations, validated within 6.6–9.6% of measurement on three physical LongCat 2.0 deployments); finds homogeneous AFD wins only in a MINORITY of studied settings and heterogeneous AFD needs deployment-level hardware complementarity, not heuristic device selection — a sober, empirically-grounded counterpoint to the "AFD is a clear win" framing, from a new independent group (Meituan) on the trend's AFD sub-axis. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 07-29 → 08-05); NO stage move. Evidence cap enforced: dropped the oldest line (NVIDIA Dynamo v1.2.0, 06-02 — the general orchestration-product datapoint, superseded by the denser AFD/AMD/vLLM evidence since).
  - 2026-07-29 — https://www.together.ai/blog/thunderagent — Together AI official blog, "ThunderAgent: 2x Faster Agentic Inference for Synthetic Data Generation at Scale" (opened this session; ICML 2026 Spotlight paper, open-source, collaboration with Georgia Tech/UIUC/CMU): treats an agentic LLM workflow as one program rather than independent requests — tracks execution phase, KV-cache footprint and node placement, then strategically pauses low-priority workflows under memory pressure (rather than evicting their KV cache) and resumes them on a node with capacity, eliminating the "KV-cache thrashing" cascade where a paused agent's cache gets evicted then fully recomputed on resume. >2x single-node throughput, 2.4x on an 8-node cluster with near-linear scaling. A new independent group (academia + Together AI) on the trend's KV-transfer/serving-efficiency axis, found via the weekly's low-cadence tier-ii capture sweep of Together AI (a source the W28-P1 amendment re-tiered to low-cadence for being "rarely on-axis" — this week it produced a genuine hit).
  - 2026-07-23 — https://rocm.blogs.amd.com/software-tools-optimization/infera-di/README.html — AMD official ROCm blog, "Infera: distributed-inference orchestration to scale goodput for agentic AI on AMD Instinct GPUs" (opened this session; open-source, github.com/AMD-AGI/Infera): an orchestration layer coordinating engine instances (vLLM/SGLang/ATOM) via THREE techniques — KV-aware routing (send requests to instances already caching the relevant prompt), Prefill-Decode Disaggregation (compute-bound prefill vs memory-bound decode on specialized instances) and KV-cache tiering (offload to NVMe/NFS via AMD Infinity Context). Up to 2.7x higher goodput/GPU on synthetic agentic workloads. A NEW independent org (AMD) building PD-disaggregation infrastructure explicitly, alongside vLLM/SGLang/NVIDIA Dynamo — found via the weekly's low-cadence tier-ii hardware capture sweep.
- notes: Stage accelerating despite no velocity history: the feature is officially documented in the major serving frameworks (vLLM, SGLang) and NVIDIA built a dedicated product on it (Dynamo). ### [id: agent-security-004] Agent security: formal limits of prompt-injection defenses and the architectural turn

(19 authors), "CodeMidas: Scaling Agentic Coding RL Environments from Code Itself" (HF daily papers, surfaced via the significance-first exploration slot; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): existing coding-RL-environment pipelines rely on development artifacts (issues, commits), limiting extractable task range; CodeMidas turns IMPLEMENTED FUNCTIONALITY in existing codebases into executable RL environments using source code as the only task-specific input — agentic compute at every construction stage (behavioral-spec formulation, execution-grounded test construction, execution-check + repeated-rollout filtering) — yielding 5,545 tasks from 3,185 open-source codebases across 23 languages/15 domains; training MiMo-V2.5 with GRPO on these tasks improves issue repair, whole-program construction AND terminal work simultaneously. WMRL replaces environment execution with a WORLD MODEL (with Online Debiasing + Inverse-Variance Denoising to offset the model's own reward bias/noise), accelerating training 3-4x while beating standard RL baselines; post-trained 4B/9B agents outperform much larger 48B/120B open-weight agents, and the method transfers to embodied VLA post-training. A genuinely new infrastructure lever on this trend's "standard interfaces between environments and trainers" axis — substituting the environment itself with a learned model rather than building a better real environment — from a new independent group. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 08-24 → 09-10, rescuing the trend from the 21-day dormancy line it was approaching); dropped the oldest line (EvoTrainer, 06-02 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. ### [id: latent-reasoning-006] Latent-space reasoning and recursive computation (looped models, latent multi-agent)
- alias: latent reasoning, continuous thought, looped transformers, RecursiveMAS, latent collaboration, RLTT
- pinned: true
- stage: emerging
- confidence: medium
- first_observed: 2026-06-11
- last_evidence: 2026-09-12
- evidence:
  - 2026-09-12 — https://github.com/yifanzhang-pro/recurrent-looped-tranformer — Yifan Zhang (independent researcher), "Recurrent Looped Transformer (RLT): Latent reasoning with infinite temporal depth" (technical report PDF + project site + repo, surfaced via the AlphaSignal curator lane, opened this session): a causal encoder builds a global key-value memory while a RECURRENT decoder combines that memory with sliding-window attention and feedback from its own previous final hidden state — so decoder state carries across every prompt/response token, extending the causal computation path linearly with sequence length while keeping the decoder's own layer count fixed (a "carry state across tokens" lever distinct from both the looped-DEPTH line, e.g. Loopie/DeepLoop, and the sequence-dimension line, e.g. Hidden Decoding, already cited). A new independent (solo-researcher) entry directly on the pinned axis; evidence is currently thin — a small synthetic experiment with mixed results, no benchmark/scaling claim yet, per AlphaSignal's own review of the report — so this is a genuinely new architectural lever to watch, not a validated result. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-09 → 09-12); dropped the oldest non-protected evidence line, Hidden Decoding (2607.08186, 07-09 — retained in the notes narrative below; RecursiveMAS 04-28 stays protected as the multi-agent-latent anchor per the 07-23 precedent) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (pinned, single early-stage report; stays emerging).
  - 2026-09-09 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10715 — The Intern-NCP Team (26 authors, incl. Dahua Lin, Zhouhan Lin), "NCP-ArchPreview Technical Report: Moving towards Latent Space Language Models through Next Concept Prediction" (HF daily papers, 91up — the day's top item; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): a latent-space language model that adds Next Concept Prediction (NCP) alongside standard next-token prediction — building a latent space via a product-quantized concept vocabulary from its own hidden states, predicting future CONCEPTS (spanning multiple tokens) via a dedicated Concept Module, then feeding predictions back to the token level. Scaled to 8.9B params on 5.73T tokens — "the largest demonstration of a latent-space language model to date" — matching OLMo-3-7B's pretraining loss using only 51.3% of the training tokens, plus a 2.45-point downstream gain. A new independent (large-consortium) group on the pinned axis via a distinct architectural lever from both the looped-depth and sequence-dimension lines already cited — concept-level latent prediction feeding back into token generation, at the largest scale yet demonstrated for a latent-space LM. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-01 → 09-09); dropped oldest evidence, "Bridging the Gap" (2606.31779, 06-30 — retained in the notes narrative below; RecursiveMAS 04-28 stays protected as the multi-agent-latent anchor per the 07-23 precedent) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (pinned, single preprint; stays emerging).
  - 2026-09-01 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01343 — Shaowen Wang, Ge Zhang, Kairong Luo, Yuhao Wu, Shaofan Liu, Jiaheng Liu et al. (9 authors), "SMELT: Scaling Laws for Compute-Matched MoE Looped Transformers" (abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session; HF daily papers, 22up): studies looping on Mixture-of-Experts Transformers while closely matching per-token FLOPs, total non-embedding parameters AND KV cache (prior looped-transformer evaluations compared at fixed model size, conflating architectural advantage with extra FLOPs) — arrives at a recipe (loop the middle half of layers twice) that beats an unlooped baseline matched on all three budgets, scaled across four sizes up to 54B non-embedding params. A new independent group on the pinned looped-transformer line, and the first to rigorously control for the FLOPs/params/KV-cache confound the prior evidence on this axis had not isolated. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 08-18 → 09-01); dropped oldest evidence (LoopWM, 06-16 — the world-model application of looped transformers, retained in this notes narrative's citation history) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (pinned, single preprint; stays emerging).
  - 2026-08-18 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17981 — Michael C. Mozer, Shoaib Ahmed Siddiqui, Danny Sawyer, Sunny Sanyal, Rosanne Liu (5 authors), "Recirculation" (arXiv abstract + metadata via export.arxiv this session, surfaced via alphaXiv trending): an inference-time architectural enhancement for OFF-THE-SHELF foundation models (no retraining) that introduces a specific form of RECURRENCE to bypass the fundamental limitation that state updates in feedforward transformers are bounded by model depth — markedly reduces perplexity and boosts accuracy on generation/reasoning tasks with essentially no added generation latency (recurrence happens in the prefill phase, processed serially). A new independent group on the pinned axis and a notably PRACTICAL entry: a drop-in inference-time technique rather than a from-scratch architecture or training recipe, applicable to any existing model. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 08-14 → 08-18); dropped oldest evidence by date, PoLar (2606.06574, 06-04 — retained in the notes narrative below; RecursiveMAS 04-28 stays protected as the multi-agent-latent anchor per the 07-23 precedent) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (pinned, single preprint; stays emerging).
  - 2026-08-14 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14290 — Kai Chen, Jifeng Ding, Ning Ding, Jiaye Ge, Lixin Gu, Yicheng Gu et al. (47 authors), "Intern-S2-Mobius: Foundation Model with Decoupled Knowledge and Reasoning" (HF daily papers, 22up; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): a new architecture (Mobius-v0) with a globally-shared MEMORY (FFN, storing knowledge vectors) and multiple REASONERS (self-attention) that iteratively query memory for needed knowledge while hidden states carry the computation as cache/carrier — "through this knowledge-reasoning-separation architecture, Mobius achieves better knowledge compression and reasoning efficiency." A NEW independent (large-consortium) group directly on the pinned axis — iterative latent computation over a shared latent memory rather than looped self-attention alone, a distinct architectural lever from both the looped-transformer line and the sequence-dimension (Hidden-Decoding) line already cited.
  - 2026-08-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09888 — Björn Engdahl, Adrian Kosowski, Jan Chorowski, Zuzanna Stamirowska, Przemysław Uznański, Junlin Jiang, Rohan Phadke, Remigiusz Kinas, Richard Zhong (9 authors, Pathway/BDH-line), "BDH-CQ: In-Context Learning with Recurrent Latent Reasoning" (arXiv abstract + metadata via export.arxiv this session; HF daily papers 08-11, 266 up — a major attention signal): a reasoning model combining in-context learning with RECURRENT LATENT reasoning — inputs continuously update a recurrent memory, and the model solves each query via iterative computation in a high-dimensional latent space WITHOUT verbalizing intermediate reasoning. Evaluated on ARC-AGI-1: a 150M-parameter configuration reaches 29.5% pass@2 at $0.0007/task, BREAKING the previously-reported ARC-AGI-1 cost-accuracy Pareto frontier — a new state of the art in benchmark cost efficiency. A new independent group on the pinned latent-reasoning axis, and the first to pair recurrent latent computation with genuine cost-efficiency SOTA on a hard reasoning benchmark rather than an architectural/scaling study. Lands the same run this trend would otherwise have crossed the 21-day dormancy line (last_evidence was 07-22 = 21d today).
  - 2026-07-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19691 — Runyang You, Zhiyuan Liu, Yongqi Li, Wenjie Li (HK PolyU-line, 4 authors), "SLPO: Scaling Latent Reasoning via a Surrogate Policy": latent reasoners (carrying intermediate computation as continuous vectors instead of decoded tokens) remain largely imitation-bound because latent trajectories lack a tractable per-step likelihood and an adaptive stopping interface, so outcome-reward RL — the recipe that moved explicit CoT past imitation — could not elicit latent test-time scaling. SLPO brings outcome-reward RL to autoregressive latent reasoners via an empirical SURROGATE policy density over latent transitions (for trajectory-level credit assignment) + a correctness-supervised stopping head refined into a variable-horizon policy; improves Pass@k under parallel sampling and allocates longer latent computation to harder instances. A new independent group on the pinned latent-reasoning line (Loopie/DeepLoop/Hidden-Decoding/LT2), and the first to give latent reasoning a working RL-scaling interface rather than an architectural trick (abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session; HF daily paper 07-23). Evidence add, NO stage move
  - 2026-07-17 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16051 — Zitian Gao, Yilong Chen, Yihao Xiao, Xinyu Yang, Ran Tao et al., "Loop the Loopies! (Loopie)": billed as the most powerful looped Transformer to date — two Mixture-of-Experts models (20B-total/2B-active and 6B-total/0.6B-active) that overcome the long-standing looped-transformer failure mode (given an N× pre-training-compute increase, N×-ing the parameter count usually beats looping a model N times). Extensive ablations INCLUDING a vanilla 30B-A3B baseline show Loopie substantially outperforms vanilla Transformer baselines trained at the SAME compute budget, plus a novel post-training pipeline. A new independent group on the pinned looped-transformer line (DeepLoop/Hidden-Decoding/Bridging-the-Gap/LoopCoder-v2/LT2), and the first to convincingly close the loop-vs-parameter-scaling gap with actually-trained MoE models rather than a training-free trick (abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session; HF daily paper 07-20, 15 up). No public weights located yet (HF search empty). Evidence add, NO stage move
  - 2026-07-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13491 — Shuzhen Li, Yifan Zhang, Jiacheng Guo, Quanquan Gu, Mengdi Wang (Princeton/UCLA-line), "DeepLoop: Depth Scaling for Looped Transformers": looped/recurrent-depth Transformers reuse weights to add computation depth without parameters, but naively deepening the loop is unstable/saturating; DeepLoop studies how to SCALE loop depth (the recurrent-depth axis) so looped models keep improving with more iterations. A new independent group on the pinned looped-transformer line (LT2/Ouro/LoopCoder-v2/PoLar/Bridging-the-Gap/Hidden-Decoding), squarely on the depth-recurrence facet (abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session; HF daily paper 07-17). Evidence add, NO stage move
  - 2026-04-28 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25917 — "Recursive Multi-Agent Systems" (Yang, Zou, Pan et al., 12 authors): the whole multi-agent system cast as one recursive latent-space computation; the RecursiveLink module transfers last-layer hidden states between heterogeneous agents instead of text; +8.3% avg accuracy, 1.2–2.4× speedup, 34.6–75.6% token reduction on 9 benchmarks
- notes: Research-side trend with direct engineering implications: token costs and latency drop when reasoning/inter-agent communication stays in hidden states. The multi-agent variant (latent state transfer instead of token messages) bridges to multi-agent-eng-009 and latent-comm-010 (the cross-model communication line specifically). Pinned per curator (a standing-watch axis). Running summary (compacted 2026-09-05 W36 per amendment W35-P2 — the pre-compaction verbatim text for 2026-06-13 through 2026-08-10 remains readable via `git log -p -- TRENDS.md`): reactivated from an initial dormancy 06-15 on PoLar (training-free per-input layer programs), then accumulated a dense, continuous run of independent groups on the looped-transformer facet — LoopCoder-v2 (trained-from-scratch 7B, 06-16), LoopWM (looped world models, 06-16), "Bridging the Gap" (06-30), Hidden Decoding (sequence-dimension latent compute as a scale-friendly alternative to depth recurrence, 07-09), DeepLoop (depth-scaling recipe, 07-15), Loopie (the first trained MoE to beat the loop-vs-parameter-scaling trade at matched compute, 07-17), SLPO (an RL-scaling interface for latent reasoners via a surrogate policy, 07-22), and BDH-CQ (recurrent latent reasoning breaking the ARC-AGI-1 cost-accuracy Pareto frontier at 150M params, 08-10, 266 HF upvotes — the strongest cost-efficiency datapoint on the axis yet). Each is a single new independent group → evidence add, no stage move (pinned, stays emerging throughout — the axis is active but no framework/open-weight release has shipped looped/latent reasoning natively). Update 2026-08-17 (daily): added Intern-S2-Mobius (2608.14290, 08-14, 47-author consortium) — a shared-latent-MEMORY + iterative-REASONER architecture, a distinct lever from both the looped-depth and sequence-dimension lines. Update 2026-08-20 (daily): added Recirculation (2608.17981, 08-18, Mozer/Siddiqui/Sawyer/Sanyal/Liu) — an inference-time, no-retraining recurrence technique applicable to any off-the-shelf model, notably PRACTICAL vs. the mostly from-scratch architectures the axis has seen. Update 2026-09-02 (daily): added SMELT (2609.01343, 09-01, HF daily papers 22up) — the first looped-transformer study to rigorously control for the FLOPs/params/KV-cache confound prior evaluations conflated, arriving at a "loop the middle half of layers twice" recipe that wins at matched budget across four sizes up to 54B. NO stage move throughout (pinned/emerging; each is a single new preprint, not framework or open-weight adoption). Watch: whether looped/latent reasoning ships natively in any open-weight release or serving engine (the standing gate toward a higher stage); whether GPT-6 Astra's reported "recurrent depth" architecture (queued, unverified as of 09-04) gets an OpenAI-side primary confirmation, which would be the first frontier-lab shipment on this exact axis. Update 2026-09-11 (daily): added NCP-ArchPreview (arXiv 2609.10715, 09-09, HF daily papers 91up) — a large-consortium group's concept-level latent-prediction architecture, the largest latent-space LM demonstrated to date (8.9B params, 5.73T tokens), a distinct lever from the looped-depth/sequence-dimension lines already tracked. last_evidence 09-01 → 09-09. ### [id: latent-comm-010] Latent-space communication between models (cache-to-cache, latent collaboration)
- alias: latent communication, C2C, KV-cache fusion, LatentMAS, model-to-model semantic transfer, Interlat
- pinned: true
- stage: emerging
- confidence: high
- first_observed: 2026-06-11
- last_evidence: 2026-09-09
- evidence:
  - 2026-09-09 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10266 — Xi Shi, Qian Lou, "KVShareArena: KV-Cache Reuse Across Contexts and Model Checkpoints" (cs.CL, abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session, found via a dedicated reactivation search): a benchmark unifying THREE separate communities' repair methods for reusing a KV cache outside the exact-prefix case — explicitly naming "a multi-agent coordinator reads reports written by other agents" as one of the two motivating workloads (the other is RAG), alongside cross-checkpoint reuse. Finds that simple position-correction suffices until a question needs several sources at once, where only re-encoding or trained-adapter methods recover half-to-two-thirds of the gap, and that cache-compression methods harmless on a single prompt fall behind on freshly-written agent reports. An ELEVENTH independent cluster (Shi/Lou, no overlap with any prior group) — a rigorous benchmark/harness for the trend's own core mechanism (reusing another agent's KV cache) rather than a new method, fires the trend's standing "reactivates on any new post-08-13-window primary" pattern. REACTIVATES: dormant-threshold (21 days since 08-31) → held at emerging by this refresh, restored/confirmed at its pre-threshold stage. last_evidence 08-31 → 09-09. Evidence cap enforced: dropped the oldest line (2606.04-vintage founding survey retained in notes narrative) — see below.
  - 2026-08-31 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00474 — Harini S, Somesh Singh, Yaman K Singla, Rajiv Ratn Shah, David Doermann, Balaji Krishnamurthy (6 authors, Adobe-line), "Exploring Collaboration between a language and a non-language agent" (accepted at EMNLP 2026; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session, found via the weekly's dedicated reactivation search): introduces LLAMIA-Bench (six collaborative chess tasks) and LATENT STATE INTERNALIZATION — projecting a non-language subagent's (a chess engine's) continuous representations directly into the LLM's token stream as learned state tokens, replacing text-verbalized inter-agent communication. Finds a consistent "verbalization debt": the performance gap between internalization and verbalized integration widens through training and persists from 4B to 14B scale; a 14B model trained this way (LLAMIA) matches or exceeds task specialists and frontier models (incl. GPT-5.1 with tool access) on all six tasks. A TENTH independent cluster — genuinely new authors (zero overlap with any of the nine prior clusters) and a distinct case (a language model communicating with a heterogeneous NON-language agent, extending the NVIDIA/Penn heterogeneous-alignment line into a concrete benchmarked system). Fires the trend's own pre-registered "reactivates immediately on any post-08-13 primary" criterion. ### [id: lowbit-quant-011] Ultra-low-bit quantization: vector and trellis coding for weights and KV cache
- alias: TCQ, trellis-coded quantization, QTIP, TurboQuant, EXL3, vector quantization, KV-cache quantization
- pinned: true
- stage: accelerating
- confidence: medium
- first_observed: 2026-06-11
- last_evidence: 2026-09-14
- evidence:
  - 2026-09-14 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16338 — Evangelos Georganas, Alexander Heinecke, Pradeep Dubey (Intel Labs, 3 authors), "Breaking the 1.58-bit Barrier for Ternary LLMs" (cs.LG, surfaced via the HN front-page pulse 171pts; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): measures the ACTUAL symbol distribution of 29 real ternary (BitNet-class) LLMs and finds zeros dominate far more than the equiprobable {-1,0,+1} assumption baked into the conventional 1.625-bit/weight five-trit packing format — the true entropy is measurably below what every shipped ternary model currently pays for storage. A NEW independent org (Intel, previously untracked on this axis) on the trend's low-bit-coding thesis, this time targeting the STORAGE-FORMAT efficiency of already-ternary models rather than a new quantization scheme for full/half-precision weights — a genuinely new sub-facet (packing-format optimality) distinct from the existing TCQ/vector-quant/recovery-recipe lines. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-04 → 09-14); dropped the oldest line (Zandieh et al.'s original TurboQuant paper, 2025-04-28 — the trend's founding artifact, still summarized at length in the notes narrative below) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/medium; a new sub-facet reinforcing the axis, not a new gate). Cross-link: small-cpu-models-008 (ternary/BitNet-class models are this trend's shared substrate).
  - 2026-09-04 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04098 — "Why Gated DeltaNet Survives 4-Bit Quantization: NVFP4 W4A4 for the Recurrent Half of a Hybrid 27B LLM" (Kozyrev & Maiboroda, arXiv metadata verified via export.arxiv this session, HF daily papers 48up): tests and overturns the community assumption that a linear-attention recurrent state (Gated DeltaNet, used in Qwen3.8-27B's hybrid attention) needs 8/16-bit precision because quantization errors compound over long contexts — ships Minima, NVFP4 W4A4 on all 496 linear layers including the GDN decay/write-strength gates. Extends the low-bit-quant frontier from static weights/KV into RECURRENT state, a previously-assumed-fragile target.
  - 2026-08-21 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20953 — Bakbergen Ryskulov, Iker García-Ferrero, David Montero, David Jansen, Ali Hashemi, Jezabel R. Garcia, Antonio Tiene, Román Orús (8 authors, Multiverse Computing-line), "Quantization-Aware Healing: A Practical Recipe for Recovering Compressed, 4-Bit LLMs" (cs.CL, HF blog pointer → followed to the arXiv primary this session; abs + metadata via export.arxiv): a NEW independent group on this trend's axis — heals a structurally-compressed model by distilling the 4-bit student directly from the original uncompressed model (not from a bf16 recovery checkpoint), converging ~7x faster than a matched QAT baseline; on a GPT-OSS 120B→60B→MXFP4 pipeline the healed student matches/beats bf16 on 7/9 benchmarks at ~4x less weight memory, released open-weight as Hypernova-60B. Distinct facet from the trend's existing TCQ/vector-quant line: a training-time recovery RECIPE for post-compression 4-bit models rather than a new coding/allocation scheme. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 08-19 → 08-21). NO stage move (accelerating/medium; reinforcement, not a new gate).
  - 2026-08-19 — https://github.com/RyanCodrai/turbovec — turbovec, an independent open-source Rust vector index built directly on Google's TurboQuant algorithm (surfaced via the HN front-page pulse, 223pts; repo README + benchmark results opened this session): applies TurboQuant's rotate-then-quantize approach (the exact technique this trend was founded on for weights/KV-cache) to a NEW domain — dense-vector similarity search — with rigorously reported benchmarks (100K-vector / 1K-query, median-of-5, ARM Google Axion + x86 Sapphire Rapids): a 10M-document corpus shrinks from 31GB (float32) to 4GB, beating FAISS FastScan on both search speed (avg 3.4-3.5x at 4-bit) and recall across every tested configuration. A NEW independent group (not Google itself) extending the TurboQuant line into a domain the trend had not previously tracked — exactly the "genuine vector/trellis/VQ primary" reactivation criterion the 07-14 dormancy note pre-registered. REACTIVATES dormant → accelerating (restored to the pre-dormancy stage, not a fresh climb, per the standing pattern for pinned-trend reactivations elsewhere in this ledger). last_evidence 06-23 → 08-19.
  - 2026-06-23 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24033 — Fengfeng Liang, Yuechen Zhang, Jiaya Jia (3 authors), "RoPE-Aware Bit Allocation for KV-Cache Quantization" (Block-GTQ): a RoPE-aware bit allocator for key-cache quantization built directly ON TurboQuant-MSE (TQ-MSE) — it decomposes a key's attention-logit contribution under RoPE into a position-dependent sum over 2-D frequency blocks, computes a label-free per-block energy score, and greedily allocates integer bit-widths by marginal gain (high-energy RoPE blocks get more bits). Cuts per-layer MAE 32–80% at 2–3 b/dim K-only quant and wins all 367/367 layer comparisons vs uniform allocation on a ten-model panel; code released. A new independent group extending the TurboQuant KV-cache-quant line this trend tracks (metadata via export.arxiv)
  - 2026-06-12 — https://docs.vllm.ai/en/latest/api/vllm/model_executor/layers/quantization/turboquant — official vLLM API docs: an in-tree `turboquant` KV-cache quantization module (Hadamard rotation + per-coordinate Lloyd-Max scalar quant for keys, uniform for values), explicitly tracing the method to TurboQuant (ICLR 2026) / DRIVE / EDEN (accessed today)
  - 2026-05-11 — https://blog.vllm.ai/2026/05/11/turboquant.html — vLLM official engine blog "A First Comprehensive Study of TurboQuant: Accuracy and Performance" (opened this session): benchmarks four in-tree variants (`--kv-cache-dtype turboquant_{k8v4,4bit_nc,k3v4_nc,3bit_nc}`) against unquantized BF16 and FP8 KV-cache baselines on 2×H100. This is the BENCHMARKED-RELEASE the trend's gate required (turboquant leaving API-only docs for an engine-measured study). HONEST finding: TurboQuant consistently UNDERPERFORMS both BF16 and FP8 on throughput/latency (FP8 matches/beats BF16 at negligible overhead); TurboQuant's value is confined to memory-constrained serving, where its compressed KV-cache cuts burst TTFT ~5× (≈17s→<3.5s) by avoiding request queuing — i.e. a capacity lever, not a speed win
  - 2026-06-11 — https://github.com/turboderp-org/exllamav3 — ExLlamaV3: the EXL3 format is a simplified variant of Cornell RelaxML's QTIP running in a production consumer-GPU inference library; v0.0.40 released June 2026, 938 stars, a 70B quantizes in hours on one RTX 4090 (accessed today)
  - 2026-04-27 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08114 — D'Alberto, "Statistical Inference and Quality Measures of KV Cache Quantisations Inspired by TurboQuant": independent statistical analysis of TurboQuant-class KV quantization (inner-product variance, softmax effects)
  - 2025-09-24 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20214 — Lee & Song (SNU), "Q-Palette: Fractional-Bit Quantizers Toward Optimal Bit Allocation" (NeurIPS 2025): fractional-bit quantizers with trellis-coded quantizers among the tools, plus joint quantizer/fusion optimization with CUDA kernels

### [id: diffusion-lm-013] Diffusion language models reach open-weights production scale

- evidence:
  - 2026-09-17 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20751 — Anton Xue, Litu Rout, Aditya Akella, Adam Klivans, Sujay Sanghavi (UT Austin-line), "dQwen3.5: Hybrid-Attention Diffusion Language Models" (cs.CL, abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session, found via a dedicated dormancy-rescue arXiv search): adapts Qwen3.5's HYBRID (attention+RNN) backbone — not the usual full-attention starting point every prior adaptation has used — into a diffusion LM family across FOUR scales (0.8B/2B/4B/9B), addressing the structural obstacle that RNN layers are causal and non-trivial to bidirectionalize; a further independent group corroborating that production-scale AR backbones (now including hybrid-attention ones) convert efficiently into diffusion LMs at multiple useful open scales. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-04 → 09-17, rescuing the trend from an 18-day approach toward the 21-day dormancy line); dropped the oldest evidence line (Sumi, 06-17 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap.   - 2026-09-04 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03796 — "LLaDA-Image: Building Strong Image Generators with Fully Open Training Recipes" (30 authors, arXiv metadata verified via export.arxiv this session, HF daily papers 64up, top item of the day): a 6B Diffusion Transformer paired with a frozen vision-language module built on the LLaDA2.0-Mini diffusion-LANGUAGE-model backbone already tracked on this trend — a distinct group extending the diffusion-LM lineage from text into image generation with a fully open (220M-sample) training recipe.
  - 2026-08-19 — https://huggingface.co/GSAI-ML/LLaDA-MoE-v2-30B-A3B-Base — GSAI-ML/Renmin University (the LLaDA line) SHIPS open weights for LLaDA MoE v2 (Base + Instruct, HF createdAt 08-19, found this weekly via the low-cadence tier-ii capture sweep of the GSAI-ML HF org): the actual downloadable checkpoint behind the already-cited scaling-laws paper (2608.03457, 08-04) — resolves that paper from a methodology study into a runnable open MoE diffusion LM, the FIFTH independent lab's open diffusion base model overall and GSAI-ML's second (after iLLaDA-8B). Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 08-04 → 08-19); NO stage move (already accelerating; completes an existing datapoint rather than firing the standing accelerating→mainstreaming gate — that still wants a SECOND diffusion base model reaching first-class native serving, or a cross-model abstraction, or non-vendor parity, none of which this fires on its own).
  - 2026-08-04 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03457 — Fengqi Zhu, Shaoxuan Xu, Jingyang Ou, Zebin You et al. (14 authors, GSAI-ML / Renmin University, the LLaDA line), "LLaDA MoE v2: Scaling Mixture-of-Experts Diffusion Language Models" (abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session, HF daily papers 08-05, 20up): the FIRST systematic characterization of how optimization hyperparameters, compute allocation and architecture SCALE for MoE diffusion LMs — reports quantitative differences from AR scaling trends (optimal nominal batch size grows faster, optimal LR decays more rapidly with compute; IsoFLOP shows a slight data-side tilt; larger scales favor larger expert pools at fixed activated capacity). A new diffusion-LM primary from the LLaDA/GSAI-ML group extending the axis from "does diffusion work" to "how do MoE dLLMs scale" — exactly the "any new diffusion-LM primary" reactivation criterion. REACTIVATES the trend: dormant → accelerating (restored to its pre-dormancy stage, not a fresh climb, consistent with the pd-disagg-002/multi-agent-eng-009 precedent). last_evidence 07-07 → 08-04.
  - 2026-07-31 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00146 — DiffusionGemma Team (43 authors, Google DeepMind-line, incl. Adrien Ali Taïga, James Assiene, Daniele Calandriello), "DiffusionGemma Technical Report" (cs.CL, arXiv abstract + metadata via export.arxiv this session; surfaced via HN 08-21, 144pts): the full technical writeup behind the already-cited DiffusionGemma model — fine-tunes Gemma 4 MoE (3.8B activated) into a discrete-diffusion LM using FEWER THAN 10% of the starting AR model's training-token budget (SFT for denoising, then RL + sampler distillation), refining blocks of 256 tokens in parallel to reach ~1,500 output tokens/sec on a single H100 (~20 tokens/forward-pass) while retaining thinking-mode, multimodal and near-AR-quality fallback generation. A concrete methodology artifact (the training recipe, not just the shipped weights) for an already-tracked model — backfill evidence dated before the existing top line, last_evidence unchanged (08-04 stays newest).
  - 2026-07-07 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05722 — NVIDIA (26 authors), "Nemotron-Labs-Diffusion: A Tri-Mode Language Model Unifying Autoregressive, Diffusion, and Self-Speculation Decoding": a SINGLE architecture trained with a JOINT AR-diffusion objective that switches among AR / diffusion / self-speculation decoding modes to sustain throughput across concurrency levels (AR supplies left-to-right linguistic priors, diffusion improves lookahead planning; in self-speculation mode diffusion beats multi-token-prediction on acceptance rate and real-device efficiency, and a speed-of-light analysis shows up to 76.5% more tokens/forward than self-speculation under an optimal sampler). Ships as OPEN WEIGHTS — nvidia/Nemotron-Labs-Diffusion-{3B,8B,14B} spanning base / instruct / vision-language (HF 8B createdAt 2026-03-18, 125k downloads, license:other, `nemotron_labs_diffusion` arch) — so the paper formalizes an already-shipped open family. The FOURTH independent lab with an open diffusion-capable base model (after Google DiffusionGemma, Tohoku Sumi, RUC iLLaDA) and the 2nd MAJOR lab (after Google) putting an open diffusion LM at production scale. Vendor-benchmarked: the 8B decodes 6× more tokens/forward than Qwen3-8B at comparable accuracy → 4× higher throughput on SPEED-Bench with SGLang on a GB200 (abs via export.arxiv this session; HF card metadata opened)

### [id: agent-eval-014] Deployment-grounded agent evaluation: long-horizon, real-session benchmarks beyond static leaderboards
- alias: agent benchmarks, long-horizon agent eval, deployment-realistic eval, predictive validity, real-session benchmarks, CEO-Bench, PlanBench, EnterpriseClawBench, UniClawBench
- stage: emerging
- confidence: high
- first_observed: 2026-06-23
- last_evidence: 2026-09-21
- evidence:
  - 2026-09-21 — https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/how-to-evaluate-ai-agents-from-tool-calls-to-task-completion/ — NVIDIA official Technical Blog, "How to Evaluate AI Agents From Tool Calls to Task Completion" (opened this session via the mandatory tier-i lab sweep): argues agent evaluation has had to shift from scoring individual function calls (BFCL-style) to scoring full task completion through executable environments that track state across multi-step tool use, proposing a fixed metric hierarchy (benchmark/trial/task/turn/step) with paired accuracy/verbosity/cost reporting and a preference for executable state-checks over reference-based or LLM-as-judge scoring; demonstrates the framework with Nemotron 3.5 Lightning (86% on PinchBench, 30% faster than comparable models). A further major-lab primary squarely on this trend's precise thesis (deployment-grounded, task-completion evaluation beyond static/call-level scoring). Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-15 → 09-21).

### [id: agent-runtime-015] Agent harness/runtime/memory as a first-class engineered, self-improving object
- alias: agent harness, harness-as-object, agent foundry, self-evolving memory, agent-native memory, agent runtime state, harness design, agent scaffolding, agent-loop infrastructure
- stage: mainstreaming
- confidence: high
- first_observed: 2026-06-27
- last_evidence: 2026-09-22
- evidence:
  - 2026-09-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26760 — Laizhen Li, Jiarui Li, Juanjuan Zhao, Kejiang Ye, Ye Li, Cheng-zhong Xu, "Grow the Harness, Not the Context: From Strategy-Free Scaffolds to Reusable Specialist Agents" (cs.AI recent-listing exploration rotation; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): "Growing Harness" is a failure-guided training paradigm that learns the agent harness ITSELF, starting from a strategy-free scaffold exposing only fixed model/tool interfaces — function-level execution traces localize each failure to a bounded code surface, an optimizer repairs a window of failures jointly, and a success-first held-out gate rolls back repairs that harm prior capability, so the harness's control structure emerges from task feedback rather than being hand-authored. A further independent group building recurring control into REUSABLE CODE rather than re-deriving it inside the model's context each task — a sharp, literal instance of this trend's own title claim. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-21 → 09-22); dropped the oldest evidence line (Repo-To-Skill, 09-02 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (mainstreaming/HIGH; reinforcement, not a new gate).

A further independent group refining the exact recursive-harness-improvement mechanism this trend tracks, this time from the failure-mode/robustness side rather than a new construction method. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-17 → 09-21); dropped the oldest line (WikiSkill, 08-27 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (mainstreaming/HIGH; reinforcement, not a new gate).

  - 2026-09-16 — https://harnesstax.github.io/ — "HarnessTax: How Much Does the Harness Matter for Coding Agents?" (UC Berkeley + Arena, surfaced via the HN front-page pulse 86pts; opened directly this session): a rigorous empirical study — 21 model×harness pairs (7 models × Claude Code/Codex CLI/Pi) on SWE-bench Lite and Terminal-Bench 2.0, 3 repetitions per task with bootstrapped confidence intervals — finds harness choice barely moves task-success rate (±2-5%) but moves COST by up to 5x for the same model/success-rate, that Pi (a minimal 4-tool open-source harness) reaches the Pareto frontier on both benchmarks, and that an alternative harness beats a model's own provider's harness in 9 of 12 Anthropic/OpenAI comparisons. A rigorous, independent (academic) empirical confirmation of this trend's own founding thesis from the OUTSIDE — the harness is not incidental to a model's measured capability, quantified for the first time across a shared benchmark rather than asserted by the shipping labs themselves. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-10 → 09-16); dropped the oldest line (AutoSaddler, 08-24 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (mainstreaming/HIGH; an independent empirical confirmation of the thesis, not a new gate). Cross-link: agent-eval-014 (deployment-grounded benchmarking methodology).

### [id: on-policy-distill-016] On-policy distillation as the post-training method for reasoning and agentic LLMs
- alias: OPD, on-policy distillation, on-policy self-distillation, student-rollout distillation, dense token-level supervision, multi-teacher distillation
- stage: accelerating
- confidence: medium
- first_observed: 2026-07-01
- last_evidence: 2026-09-22
- evidence:
  - 2026-09-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26708 — Yuanteng Chen, Zhilei Liu, Peisong Wang, Yuantian Shao, Chuangyi Li, Weining Wang, "Train Where the Quantized Model Goes: On-Policy Distillation for Low-Bit Reasoning" (cs.AI recent-listing exploration rotation; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): identifies quantization-amplified exposure bias — standard quantization-aware distillation (QAD) trains on fixed corpus prefixes while quantization-induced deviations compound along the model's OWN autoregressive trajectory at deployment, causing sub-3-bit models' long-generation reasoning to degenerate into repetitive loops — and fixes it with an OPD stage where, starting from a QAD checkpoint, the student generates through its actual quantized forward path and receives dense token-level feedback from a frozen full-precision teacher on its OWN prefixes. A genuinely new application of this trend's core method (place teacher supervision where the model actually goes) to the low-bit-quantization axis specifically, bridging this trend with lowbit-quant-011. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-21 → 09-22); dropped the oldest evidence line ("Beyond Imitation", 08-19 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/medium; reinforcement via a new application domain, not a new gate). Cross-link: lowbit-quant-011.
  - 2026-09-21 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24432 — Huanxin Sheng, Zhiling Ye, Haonan Wang et al., "1% of Tokens Can Be Enough: On Gradient Estimation in On-Policy Distillation" (HF daily papers; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): a further independent refinement of the trend's own core supervision signal (alongside Cal-OPD's teacher-self-deviation calibration) — studies sparse OPD's gradient-estimation problem in information geometry, proposing an information-efficiency ratio (IER, a signal-to-noise decomposition under an optimal scalar baseline) that selects which ~1% of tokens carry genuinely useful, low-noise teacher gradient signal rather than allocating supervision uniformly or by a heuristic. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-18 → 09-21); dropped the oldest line (Open-MOPD, 08-19 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/medium; reinforcement of the signal-quality/token-selection facet, not a new gate).
  - 2026-09-18 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21619 — Qiangqiang He, Jin Li, MingCai Chen (3 authors), "Calibrating Teacher–Student Discrepancy for On-Policy Distillation" (Cal-OPD; HF daily papers, abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session, found via the significance-first exploration slot): names a mechanism-level flaw in standard OPD — the observed teacher-student token-level discrepancy does not purely reflect the capability gap; it also mixes in the teacher's OWN self-deviation (exacerbated under privileged OPD, where privileged information shifts the teacher's own likelihoods), and standard OPD indiscriminately learns all of it. Cal-OPD estimates the teacher's self-deviation region via positive/negative privileged interventions and calibrates the discrepancy to keep only the genuine capability-gap component — retaining just ~52-65% of the original signal while consistently outperforming standard OPD and its variants on math-reasoning benchmarks across model scales. A further independent group directly refining the trend's own core supervision signal (which part of the teacher-student gap is worth learning), distinct from Open-MOPD's routing-ambiguity diagnosis and "Beyond Imitation"'s progress-filtering approach. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-09 → 09-18); dropped the oldest line ("Every Coin Has Two Sides," 08-17 — the trend's dual-nature-generalization limits finding, still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/medium; reinforcement of the signal-quality facet, not a new gate).
  - 2026-09-09 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03241 — "FlowBalance: Verifier-Grounded Self-Improvement from On-Policy Reasoning Experience" (arXiv metadata verified via export.arxiv this session, HF daily papers 83up — the day's #2 item): a further independent group extending the trend's teacher-free/verifier-grounded self-improvement facet — the student's own on-policy reasoning experience, checked against a verifier, drives the improvement signal rather than a separate teacher model. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-07 → 09-09); dropped the oldest line (NVIDIA's open multi-tier OPD teacher-stack release, 08-14 — still summarized at length in this trend's notes narrative, including its role in the accelerating-stage promotion) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/medium; reinforcement of the teacher-free facet, not a new gate).
  - 2026-09-07 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05295 — Yang Li, Semih Yavuz, Shafiq Joty (Salesforce AI Research), "RISE: Recursive Improvement via Self-Extrapolating Policy Distillation" (arXiv metadata verified via export.arxiv this session, HF daily papers): names OPD's own bottleneck precisely — external teachers suffer distribution mismatch, while self-distillation with privileged conditioning is capped by in-context learning capacity — and constructs a SYNTHETIC teacher directly by extrapolating the displacement between the current checkpoint and its own RLVR training trajectory, rather than relying on an external or privileged-context teacher. A further independent group on the trend's teacher-free/self-evolving facet (alongside Self-OPD, Latent On-Policy Self-Distillation), extending it from a fixed synthetic-teacher construction rather than removing the teacher outright. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-04 → 09-07); dropped the oldest line (Latent On-Policy Self-Distillation, 08-13 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/medium; reinforcement of the teacher-free facet, not a new gate).
  - 2026-09-04 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04172 — "Rethinking On-Policy Distillation of Large Language Models II: One Training Example" (Fu, He, Zuo et al., 13 authors, arXiv metadata verified via export.arxiv this session, HF daily papers 36up): a direct continuation of the on-policy-distillation line already tracked on this trend — at the data-minimal limit (a SINGLE training query), one-shot OPD keeps improving for hundreds of steps and recovers most of full-data OPD's gain across task domains/model families, isolating the effect to the states visited during training rather than data diversity.
  - 2026-08-31 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31046 — Yi Ding, Ruqi Zhang (Purdue), "Does On-Policy Distillation Really Distill? From Noisy Teacher to Self-Improvement" (surfaced via the HF daily-papers exploration slot, 15up; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): a critical mechanistic analysis of OPD itself — quantifies substantial NOISE in the teacher's token-level supervision (the teacher scores student-generated trajectories that are inherently off-policy for it), showing the reliability of OPD's dense supervision, and hence the true source of the student's improvement, is less clear-cut than assumed. A new independent group interrogating the axis's own core mechanism rather than proposing another OPD variant. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 08-27 → 08-31); evidence cap enforced: dropped the oldest line (U-OPSD, 08-06 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative). ### [id: agentic-rl-credit-017] Dense credit assignment and process supervision for long-horizon agentic RL (beyond sparse outcome rewards)
- alias: credit assignment, turn-level credit, role-typed credit, process supervision, dense supervision, agentic RL rewards, outcome-reward limits, verification bottleneck, GRPO advantage, reward sparsity
- stage: emerging
- confidence: medium
- first_observed: 2026-07-02
- last_evidence: 2026-09-20
- evidence:
  - 2026-09-20 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23808 — Jingxuan Xu, Gang Wu, Yanan Wu, Yutao Mou et al. (16 authors), "FLARE: A Full-Lifecycle Dense Supervision Paradigm for Long-Horizon Coding Agents via Generative Reward Model" (cs.SE, abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session, found via a dedicated dormancy-rescue search): names the same credit-assignment crisis this trend tracks — sparse binary (Pass/Fail) rewards in long-horizon SWE agents waste failed exploratory trajectories — and proposes RADAR, an offline causal-chain-backtracking diagnostic that distills a lightweight Generative Reward Model (GRM) giving real-time step-level risk feedback; the GRM then serves BOTH as an inference-time "Active Scaffold" (intercepting high-risk steps for localized re-execution) and as a training-time dense-reward signal for SFT/RL (19.13% SFT gain, 9.19% RL gain in sparse environments). A further independent group on the turn/step-level dense-credit mechanism (alongside ECHO/CAST/ABSeeker/ARC/PGPO), distinct in applying it end-to-end across both inference and post-training rather than one phase. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-02 → 09-20, rescuing the trend one day before the 21-day dormancy line); dropped the oldest line (ECHO, 06-29 — one of the trend's founding groups, stays named in this notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (emerging/medium; reinforcement of the existing credit-assignment mechanism, not a new gate).
  - 2026-09-02 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02236 — Yuyao Zheng, Haipeng Sun, Junwei Bao, Lemao Liu, Hongfei Jiang, Yang Song, Dejing Dou (7 authors), "PGPO: Potential-Guided Policy Optimization for Multi-Turn Agentic Tasks" (cs.CL, abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session, found via a dedicated dormancy-rescue search): names this trend's exact bottleneck in existing step-level fixes — GiGPO's step-level advantages still rely on each trajectory's own final outcome, so actions inside FAILED trajectories stay poorly differentiated (an effective action gets the same unfavorable credit as an erroneous one). PGPO estimates empirical STATE POTENTIALS from anchor-state-group return statistics across an entire rollout group, then derives action advantages from potential DIFFERENCES between adjacent states — enabling cross-trajectory credit propagation instead of within-trajectory-only credit. A further independent group directly extending the alias's "GRPO advantage" mechanism with a structural fix, distinct from ABSeeker/ECHO/CAST/ARC's step-level formulations. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 08-31 → 09-02, refreshing the trend before the 21-day dormancy line); dropped the oldest line (Alibaba/Qwen-line's "Verification Horizon" diagnosis, 06-24 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (emerging/medium; a further group on the existing credit-assignment mechanism, reinforcing rather than newly clearing the promotion gate). A same-batch corroborator was found and reviewed but not added this pass (avoiding two same-run evidence lines): "Dense Process Supervision for Search Agents via Fact Utility Estimation" (arXiv 2609.00833, 09-01, 10 authors) — models search-agent reasoning as accumulated discrete evidence facts, directly on the process-supervision half of the alias; queued below-bar, watch for recurrence.
  - 2026-08-31 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31075 — Zhiqin Yang, Jingwen Fu, Yuhan Liu, Hengyu Liu, Yonggang Zhang, Kainan Cao, "Scaling Large Reasoning Models beyond Human Supervision: A Path toward Superintelligence" (surfaced via the HF daily-papers exploration slot, 15up; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): argues that extending RLVR's gains from math/code (automatically-checkable outcomes) to open-ended agentic tasks is blocked precisely by this trend's own bottleneck — reliable rewards are harder to obtain, and direct human supervision cannot keep pace with model capability growth. A new independent group naming and motivating the same verification-bottleneck/reward-sparsity problem from a scaling-strategy angle. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 08-24 → 08-31); evidence cap enforced: dropped the oldest line (CASIA collapse-and-fix diagnosis, 06-24 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative). NO stage move (seed/medium; reinforces the trend's founding problem statement, not a new mechanism).
  - 2026-08-24 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23318 — Zixuan Wang, Yanrui Miao, Zhengxi Lu, Teng Pan et al., "Agent-G$^2$: Gaussian Guidance for Agentic Reinforcement Learning" (HF daily papers, 15up; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): addresses hint-based RL for reward sparsity in long-horizon agentic tasks (retaining a prefix of an expert trajectory before rollout so the policy explores from a state closer to success) — prior methods treat the guidance DEPTH (how much trajectory to keep) as a deterministic scalar; this paper reframes it as a distribution to be learned per-state, directly on the trend's "how much credit/guidance should a given state get" thesis. A further independent group on the dense-supervision-for-long-horizon-RL axis, distinct mechanism (guidance-depth-as-distribution) from the trend's existing GRPO-advantage-correction and turn-level-credit lines. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 08-13 → 08-24); no cap issue (9 → 10 items). NO stage move (seed/medium; reinforces the existing axis, does not by itself clear the trend's standing promotion gate).
  - 2026-08-13 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13622 — Yongqi Tong, Tan Li Hui Faith, Choy Zhen Wen Marcus, Zhou Jin et al. (7 authors), "ARC: Fair Relative Advantage Comparison in Open-Ended Real-World Interaction" (cs.CL, HF daily papers 08-25, 4 up, backfill; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): open-ended interaction admits multiple valid behaviors (answer directly, ask for clarification, provide a progress update, confirm before acting), which breaks group-based RL's core assumption that rollouts compared within a group are behaviorally comparable — reward-model preferences over interaction STYLE distort the relative ADVANTAGE computation (the alias's own "GRPO advantage" term) and steer optimization toward reward-preferred behaviors rather than context-appropriate ones; proposes a fairness correction so advantage comparisons only happen across behaviorally-comparable rollouts. Directly on this trend's core claim (outcome/preference rewards are an incomplete proxy, and naive advantage computation needs a structural fix) via a distinct mechanism (behavioral-comparability filtering) from ABSeeker/ECHO/CAST's step-level backtracking approaches. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 08-05 → 08-13, resolving a 20-day near-dormancy gap); NO stage move (still seed; a fourth independent angle on the mechanism, reinforcing rather than newly clearing the shared-interface promotion gate — flagged again for the weekly).
  - 2026-08-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05102 — Yijun Lu, Rui Ye, Jiajun Wang, Yuwen Du, Tian Jin et al., "ABSeeker: Training Long-Horizon Search Agents via Answer-Backtracked Credit Assignment" (cs.CL, HF daily papers 08-07, 57 up — top item of the day; arXiv metadata via export.arxiv this session): converts sparse trajectory-level outcomes into DENSE step-level supervision by tracing BACKWARD from the ground-truth answer to recover the intermediate clues a correct trajectory needed (Answer-Backtracked Clue Recovery), then scoring each search step against those clues (Clue-Anchored Step Scoring) — rewards useful actions even inside failed trajectories, suppresses redundant/erroneous ones; trained ABSeeker on Qwen3.5-4B with only 8.5k examples, matching ~30B-scale agents on BrowseComp/BrowseComp-ZH. A THIRD independent group specifically on the alias's "turn-level credit" / step-level mechanism (alongside ECHO's epistemic belief-state formulation and CAST's game-solver-teacher formulation) — via a third distinct implementation (answer-backtracking). Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 07-28 → 08-05); NO stage move — three groups now converge on the SAME specific mechanism (step/turn-level dense credit) but via three different implementations with no shared interface or benchmark, short of the promotion gate's "shared interface/benchmark" bar as written; flagged prominently for the weekly to judge whether 3 groups on one mechanism (even without a shared interface) now clears emerging.

(11 authors), "CAST: Game Solvers as Turn-Level Teachers for LLM Agents" (surfaced via HF daily papers 07-30, arXiv metadata via export.arxiv this session): RLVR on long-horizon games relies on sparse final rewards that reveal little about which decisions determined success; CAST uses GAME SOLVERS as turn-level teachers to supply dense, per-decision credit — a second independent group on the alias's "turn-level credit" mechanism specifically (alongside ECHO's epistemic-decision-process turn-level credit, 06-29), via a distinct implementation (solver-as-teacher vs belief-state formulation). (7 authors, MSR-line), "LLM-as-a-Coach: Experiential Learning for Non-Verifiable Tasks": RL on open-ended tasks compresses a rubric-based evaluation into a SCALAR reward, discarding rich textual feedback and conflating responses with distinct quality profiles. Experiential Learning (EL) repurposes the feedback model from an LLM-as-a-Judge into an LLM-as-a-Coach that distills its assessment of each on-policy response into transferable experiential knowledge, which conditions a teacher and is internalized by the policy through on-policy context distillation — a HIGHER-BANDWIDTH feedback channel than the scalar outcome reward. Clarke, Yichuan Zhang, José Miguel Hernández-Lobato, "Infinite-Parameter LLMs: Generating and Adapting Weights from Live Data" (HN front page 131pts; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session, found via a dedicated dormancy-rescue check — this trend was at 19 days without evidence, crossing the 21-day dormancy line ~09-18): MoE architectures activate a fraction of a large but STATIC, pretrained parameter bank per token; this work instead GENERATES/adapts weights on the fly from live/streaming data the model encounters after pretraining, compiling what would otherwise need to sit in context (or be relearned via fine-tuning) directly into freshly-produced parameters instead. A SIXTH independent group on the compile-knowledge/behavior-into-weights axis, and a distinct sub-facet from the trend's existing evidence (DMoE/Skill-to-LoRA/PAW/Macaron-V1/FCPRAG all compile a FIXED target into weights once; this one continuously regenerates weights from data the model has not yet seen at train time). (6 authors, Harvard-line), "Program-as-Weights: A Programming Paradigm for Fuzzy Functions": everyday tasks that resist clean rule-based code (alerting on important log lines, repairing malformed JSON, ranking by intent) are increasingly outsourced to LLM APIs at the cost of locality, reproducibility and price; PAW instead COMPILES such a "fuzzy function" from a natural-language spec into a compact, locally-executable neural artifact — a 4B compiler (trained on the released 10M-example FuzzyBench) emits parameter-efficient ADAPTERS for a frozen, lightweight model. Behavior specified in text → compiled into weights for a frozen base; the 3rd independent group on this claim (top HF daily paper 2026-07-03, 27 up; abs opened this session)
  - 2026-06-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16769 — Tianyi Zhang, Zhonghao Qi (CUHK-line), "Skill-to-LoRA: From Using Skills to Learning Behaviors for Token-Efficient LLM Agents": agent skills are distributed as SKILL.md procedural documents that must be repeatedly injected into the runtime CONTEXT; S2L replaces runtime skill TEXT with skill-specific LoRA ADAPTERS that model the behavioral change the skill induces (not compression of the document), cutting token overhead while preserving task performance — "from using skills to learning behaviors" (abs re-opened this session)
  - 2026-06-12 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14243 — Baoqing Yue, Weihang Su, Qingyao Ai, Yichen Tang, Changyue Wang et al. ### [id: agentic-ai-research-019] AI agents conducting open-ended AI/scientific research: measuring, and building for, autonomous discovery
- alias: AI Scientist, autonomous research agents, recursive self-improvement (data-centric), open-ended research capability, verifiable-research frameworks

  - 2026-09-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26457 — Dhruv Srikanth, Bingchen Zhao, Dixing Xu, Yuxiang Wu, Zhengyao Jiang, "Recursive self-improvement of AI research agents" (cs.AI recent-listing exploration rotation; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): AIDE² proposes changes to its OWN code, benchmarks the modified agent on a suite of AI R&D tasks, and keeps only changes that improve hidden-evaluation performance — in an autonomous 8-day run it discovered seven successive self-improvements (a new search policy, memory-compaction mechanisms), which then GENERALIZE to four held-out benchmarks (ML engineering, heuristic algorithm engineering, and out-of-distribution physics-based weather forecasting), matching or exceeding a human-engineered production research agent on FML-Bench. A rigorous, multi-benchmark demonstration of exactly this trend's "building for autonomous discovery" facet, with generalization evidence (not just in-distribution gains) distinguishing it from most prior single-benchmark case studies. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-17 → 09-22).

As a warm-up the same effort's agents independently resolved the (unforced) Euler regularity disproof (~100 agents, ~50 hours) before redirecting the bulk of resources to Navier–Stokes (resolved ~88 hours after launch; Lean verification +17h via GPT-6 Astra; 2.7M inter-agent messages / ~130B output tokens on this problem alone). OpenAI states it does not intend to claim the prize and frames the release explicitly as a report on AI progress pace, not a culmination.
